# MARL E3 — self-audit ledger (started 28/09/2026 13:05)

This ledger is the working record of the worker's self-audit of its OWN E3 code, Lean and documents
(AGENTS.md result-to-algorithm audit). It is append-only: an entry is never deleted.
- An entry that turns out not to be a defect is marked **WITHDRAWN**, with the reason.
- A repeat of an earlier defect is marked **DUP of L-xx**.
- It does not replace `MARL_CURRENT_CLAIMS_20260928.md`, which is the current claim map, and it does
  not edit ROADMAP / OPEN_MATH / THEOREM_MAP.

**Types**
- **BUG**: the implementation contradicts its own specification or theorem.
- **OVERCLAIM**: text says more than the evidence.
- **STALE**: text contradicts newer results.
- **PROV**: provenance gap.
- **STAT**: missing uncertainty or sensitivity.
- **RISK**: a latent failure that has not occurred yet.
- **OK**: inspected, no defect.

**Status values**
- **open**
- **fixing** (what is running is named)
- **fixed** (where)
- **WITHDRAWN**
- **DUP**

**Cost to fix, in tokens**
- **H**: a rewrite or a long read.
- **M**: a new script or version.
- **L**: a line edit.

| L-81 (01:04) | decisive control: information vs action perturbation (`MARL_e3_forced_action_probe.py`, `MARL_e3_forced_action_report.py`, `e3_forced_action_report_20260929.txt`; scratch s3, 48 DEV states, 3-step label scale; self-checks passed) | **Message candidates that change exactly one agent's action at t: oracle +11.02 [+8.46, +13.60], mean +0.01.** Forcing one agent's action with NO message change (63 per state): oracle +11.96 [+10.19, +13.54]; best-of-N matched to the message set +11.27 [+9.58, +12.81]; mean −3.08. Paired, same agent and same action: message − forced −0.54 [−1.23, +0.09], message better in 22% of pairs. Message candidates that change no action at t: oracle +5.40, mean +0.15. **Reading: the E3 oracle headroom is, to first order, best-of-N headroom over action perturbations of an imperfect actor.** Blind forcing reaches the same oracle, and reaching an action through a message is not better than forcing it. What messages add is which perturbations occur: the actor's own plausible alternatives, mean about 0 instead of −3. They do not make the chosen change better. The purely message-carried part (no action change at t) is about a third of the oracle, and it is itself a best-of-N over a zero-mean spread. Scope: one actor; single-agent changes only (the best message candidates change 1.3–2.3 agents, overall oracle +15.4); 3-step scale. Consistent in spirit with `LeanMarlProxyBlindnessV1` (revision power suffices, first-order information does not), which is a different, subset-level statement and does not prove this. |

(3) Every critic (v1–v8, legal and privileged) uses the same architecture: a GLOBAL context embedding concatenated with a state-independent candidate matrix, so the value of an edit (i, +j, −k) must be learned from a flat 1,180-dim context; train 0.63–0.70 vs DEV 0.50 is heavy overfitting. Untested, but judged unlikely to rescue: the privileged critic, which gets each candidate's induced joint action (the feature that explains 74–81% of g3, L-81), still reaches only 0.55–0.57. (4) The theorems T1/BlockFrontier/SafeEdit are about a fixed value table (the 3-step label). The code never estimates ε, u, δ, η, ν or ζ; the manuscript says so. No hidden claim found. (5) S1's clamping check covers the critic only (other session). |

The scheduler's packet of agent i is one decision old (age 10 s), and the learned actor changes phase at almost every decision (elapsed 10 s throughout), so the stale phase is almost always wrong; true-vs-packet phase is a broad confusion, not a permutation. The scheduler lacks the receiver's current phase, which is private and changes every step. |

The prefix delivers senders by the scheduler's cached waiting (`SchedulerContext.cached_queue_ranking`), so the actor's per-sender `fresh` flag is a congestion cue: waiting in the content of fresh senders 7.93 vs 3.05 for cached heard senders. Pattern-only perturbations, content unchanged, change 9–21% of actions: all fresh=0 15.8%; age=0 9.3%; all heard marked fresh 18.6%; fresh/age pattern permuted among heard senders 20.7%. Content permuted with the pattern kept: 42.6%. Reading: the actor uses content most, but a fifth of its decisions also depend on WHICH senders arrived fresh. An edit that swaps sender k for j therefore also tells receiver r "j is congested" through the flag, a cue the actor learned under the prefix. Consistent with msguse (full delivery hurts prefix-trained actors) and with random edits being harmful. Not shown: realistic single-swap perturbations (these are whole-view perturbations, out of distribution); other actors. | diagnostic, one actor |

| L-165 (13:3x, 30/09) | AUDIT of the relay byte accounting (an external review, pasted by the user 13:2x; verified by recomputation) | **Confirmed specification gap.** `models/marl_relay.py` states "1 B header + 8 × 3 B = 25 B, one slot per other agent". A broadcast relay packet must carry 9 summaries (its sender plus the 8 other agents; every receiver needs a different subset). At L = 4 each summary is 21 bits (sender id 4 + phase 4 + elapsed 1 + vehicles 2 × 4 + waiting 1 × 4), so 189 bits plus 8 relay ages, above 200 bits. The simulation also gives the receiver EXACT ages. So the claim "identical byte accounting" (L-162, L-163) is NOT supported as stated. A feasible layout: positional slots (slot = agent id, so no id bits) of 17 bits × 9 = 153 bits, plus 8 saturating 4-bit ages (0 … 15 decision steps) = 185 bits ≤ 200. Every L-163 result must be re-measured with ages SATURATED at 15 steps before the accounting claim can stand. The age diagnostic found ages ≥ 4 steps in 9.7 % of relayed summaries under the prefix, with a max of 24, so saturation binds only rarely. Status: a spec gap, not yet a demonstrated change of results; fix and re-evaluation queued. | correction pending re-measurement |

It can give the channel model a THEOREM that RLM and incremental optimisation do not: the worst-capacity regret of any law over broadcast orders, and its collapse when every packet is complete (relay). (b) **ADAPTIVE tracking** (`LeanAdaptiveTrackingV1`: `exists_deficient`, `tracking_bound`, `tracking_exists`, deficit ≤ 1). The age-first scheduler is the greedy most-deficient tracking rule with uniform targets. With K_i ≥ 1 (every receiver decodes the stream head), it bounds every sender's head-gap, hence the relayed age. (c) **FUNCTIONAL certificate lifetime** (`LeanFunctionalCertificateLifetimeV1` LIFE1–LIFE5) plus the MARL module's `lifetime_from_drift_rate`, `argmax_stable_lipschitz` and `certified_skip_keeps_trajectory`. (e) **MASTER**: a regret floor for the no-evidence policy plus a priced acquisition law with a STATED comparator. That is the frame for the distilled 0-byte student as the floor of communication-free policies. (f) **SUPPORT / N5**: decide without identifying; the resolution theorem (tolerance met below a critical radius). This is the frame for 2–3 B decision summaries. Nothing of that was run yet (L-164: nothing launched). Fix: `models/marl_delivery_music.py` (new) applies cap<c> under every order. **Music reading of the channel:** under prefix decoding position 1 is heard by EVERY receiver (the BASS line), later positions only by larger K (upper voices of an arrangement for an ensemble of unknown size); with relay packets the head position decides WHICH TABLE everybody gets, while the other positions inject fresh summaries in turn (HOCKET). Prior art for the principle: PET (Albanese et al. 1994), RLM (McCanne 1996), layered coding (L-166); maximal evenness / Euclidean rhythms (Clough–Douthett; Toussaint) = AoI round-robin; the age-first order = ADAPTIVE greedy tracking (L-167 b). New rule **bass**: position 1 = the agent whose relay table is freshest (least total age), the rest agefirst. Legal: tables are a deterministic function of past orders, revealed K, submitted sets and measurement times; `AgeMirror` replays them from those records and the evaluator asserts equality with the merged views at every step. **Traffic-free age simulator** (scratchpad `relay_age_sim.py`, 100 episodes × 26 steps, m = 9; calibrated: its sticky order gives ages ≥ 4 steps 6.7 %, max 24, agefirst ages ≥ 2 steps 7.5 %, vs the real L-164 smoke 9.7 % / max 24 and 6.6 %): iid K ~ U{1..7}: P(age ≥ 2) agefirst 7.5 % → bass 2.4 %, max age 6 → 3; low K ~ U{1..3}: P(age ≥ 4) 5.3 % → 0.4 %, max 8 → 5; cap 4: a sticky order leaves 47 % of (receiver, agent) pairs NEVER covered, agefirst and bass cover 99.97 %. A greedy expected-age 'voicing' order equals bass (iid P(age ≥ 2) 2.4 %). **Eval** `scripts/MARL_e3_delivery_eval_relay_v3.py` (new; relay eval + --order prefix/agefirst/bass). Smoke (relay_st_s0, 901–902 × 8/16): --order prefix reproduces the relay eval rows EXACTLY for prefix and cap4 (queue and bytes, 8/8 episodes); mirror check 208/208 steps for every order. Byte note: under the prefix rule the stream length depends on the order (agefirst/bass +0.5 … +1.4 % bytes in the smoke); under cap4 bytes are identical (2600/episode). **Pre-registered NOW, before the panel run** (development panel 901–964 × 8/16, relay_st_s0 and relay_st_s1, --relay age --quant 4, exact ages as in L-163; watchdog): PRIMARY bass vs the prefix order of the same actor under cap4 (iid K) AND under the low capacity process (K ~ U{1..3}), paired waiting, bootstrap by traffic seed: 'bass helps at low capacity' iff the upper 95 % bound < 0 on BOTH seeds in BOTH conditions. Secondary: bass vs cap-correct agefirst; bass under iid prefix within ±1 % of the prefix order; each vs the same-seed 0-byte student (its existing 'none' rows). A development result only; a confirmatory test needs a new unused panel and its own protocol. Also still owed (L-165): re-measure with ages saturated at 15 steps. | pre-registered; running |

| L-181 (19:2x, 30/09) | MATH of the prefix channel (user 19:1x: "focus on the math"). Traffic-free replica with the E3 `prefix_sets` and the E3 `AgeMirror` (transient relay merge, the one asserted equal to the evaluator's merged views); scratchpad math_sim.py / math_sim2.py / math_lock.py / math_hub.py. | **(1) Relay memory is redundant under prefix decoding.** A persistent (gossip) merge that also keeps a receiver's own relayed copies changes mean ages by ≤ 0.01 step in every condition (iid 0.58 / 0.57; low 1.42 / 1.41). Reason: decoded sets are prefixes of one order, hence NESTED. The largest-capacity receiver knows what any receiver knew, and every receiver decodes the head. **(2) Injection floor.** With at most L senders transmitted per step, the r-th freshest sender has age ≥ ⌊(r−1)/L⌋, so the mean injection age is ≥ F(m, L) = (1/m) Σ_s s·min(L, m − sL). Age-first attains it exactly: measured 1.78 / 1.00 / 0.67 / 0.67 for constant K = 1 / 2 / 3 and cap4 = F(9, 2) / F(9, 3) / F(9, 4) / F(9, 4). Receiver ages exceed the floor by a propagation gap (iid +0.42, cap4 +0.18, low +0.65), which head rules barely change (bass: low 1.42 → 1.29). This explains why bass and the deadline head do not beat age-first on E3 capacities. **(3) Phase-lock law (raw packets, constant K, age-first, index ties).** When L = K + 1 divides m, the never-covered share is EXACTLY (m − L)/(L(m − 1)) (all 12 divisible cases, m = 4 … 13). Non-divisible cases also lock, at smaller values. So the conjecture "lock iff L divides m" is FALSE. With random tie-breaks: 0 in every case. **(4) Relay equalises heterogeneous capacity, one hop late.** At equal stream length, one receiver with K = k and the rest K = 1, relay + a dominant-knower head (last step's largest-capacity receiver), gives ages 2.35 / 1.60 / 1.26 / 0.99 / 0.78 for k = 2 / 3 / 4 / 6 / 8. That is the homogeneous all-K = k value plus ≈ 0.7 step (one hop for the K = 1 receivers). Raw packets: 4.4 / 4.8 / 4.3 / 3.9 / 3.3. Age-first head: 3.05 / 2.50 / 1.87 / 1.35 / 0.84. **PREDICTIONS for CityFlow, written BEFORE any run (condition rich7: each step one seeded receiver K = 7, others K = 1; eval-only, development panel, relay_st_s0/s1, same-seed zero-byte student):** P1 relay + age-first beats the zero-byte student on both seeds (upper bound < 0). P2 the dominant-knower (hub) head is at least as good as age-first, and better on at least one seed (point estimate < −0.3 % vs age-first). P3 FO (raw, fresh-only teacher) loses to relay + age-first by more than it does under iid on both seeds. | math: (1), (2), (4) numerical in the replica, with a proof of the floor inequality; (3) exact formula on the divisible cases, conjecture refuted; predictions pre-run |
